Suppose that the conjugation action of a section
u
of

\mathbf{S\Gamma}(\mathcal{E},q)
takes an isotropic subbundle

\mathcal{W}\subseteq\mathcal{E}
to
\mathcal{V}
. Letting the special
Clifford group act on the right,

\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{W}}\cdot u^{-1}=\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)\cdot(u\cdot\det\mathcal{W}\cdot u^{-1})=\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)\cdot\det\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{V}}.

Thus the action of
u
provides an isomorphism

\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{W}}\cong\mathcal{I}^{\mathcal{V}}
of

\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)
-modules, whence an isomorphism

\mathcal{S}^{\mathcal{W}}\cong\mathcal{S}^{\mathcal{V}}

of the corresponding spinor sheaves.

As an application of this principle which will be useful in
§
7
, the following shows that, over an
algebraically closed field, once the quadric is at least
4
-dimensional and
its singular locus is at most
1
-dimensional, then a given spinor sheaf is
isomorphic to the spinor sheaf associated with a linear space containing any
given smooth point:

5.10 Lemma
. —

Let
Q\subset\mathbf{P}V
be a quadric of dimension
n-1\geq 4
and corank

\leq 2
over an algebraically closed field
\mathbf{k}
. Write
n
as

2\ell+1
or
2\ell+2
. Given any smooth point
x\in Q
and any

\ell
-plane
\mathbf{P}W\subset Q
, there exists an
\ell
-plane

\mathbf{P}W^{\prime}\subset Q
containing
x
whose associated spinor sheaf is
isomorphic to that of
\mathbf{P}W
.

Proof.

If
x\in\mathbf{P}W
, then there is nothing to show. So suppose that

x\notin\mathbf{P}W
. Then it suffices to show that there is

x^{\prime}\in\mathbf{P}W
which is a smooth point of
Q
in the orbit of
x
under
the special Clifford group: If
g\cdot x=x^{\prime}
for
g\in\mathbf{S\Gamma}(V,q)
,
then
\mathbf{P}W^{\prime}\coloneqq g^{-1}\cdot\mathbf{P}W\subset Q
is an
\ell
-plane
containing
x
whose spinor sheaf is isomorphic to that of
\mathbf{P}W
by

5.9
. Construct
x^{\prime}
in three steps:

First, there exists a point
x^{\prime}\in\mathbf{P}W
which is a smooth point of
Q

and such that the line in
\mathbf{P}V
spanned by
x
and
x^{\prime}
is disjoint
from the singular locus of
Q
: Writing
v\in V
for a basis vector of
the linear space underlying
x
, this means there exists
v^{\prime}\in W

not contained in
\mathbf{k}\cdot v+\rad q
; in other words, it suffices to
show that

(\mathbf{k}\cdot v+\rad q)\cap W\subsetneq W.

Since
\dim_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbf{k}\cdot v+\rad q)\leq 3
and

\dim_{\mathbf{k}}W=\ell+1\geq 3
, the only way equality can occur is if

\mathbf{k}\cdot v+\rad q=W
. This is impossible since
v\not\in W
.
Now fix any such
x^{\prime}=\mathbf{P}v^{\prime}\in\mathbf{P}W
.

Second, there is a
4
-dimensional subspace
U\subset V
such that

q\rvert_{U}
is non-degenerate and
v,v^{\prime}\in U
. Set

U_{1}\coloneqq\mathbf{k}\cdot v\oplus\mathbf{k}\cdot v^{\prime}\subset V
. There are then two
cases depending on the pairing between
v
and
v^{\prime}
: If
b_{q}(v,v^{\prime})=0
,
then
U_{1}
is isotropic and intersects
\rad q
trivially, so by the theory of
hyperbolic planes, there is a subspace
U_{2}\subset V
mapped isomorphically to

U_{1}^{\vee}
by
b_{q}
. If
b_{q}(v,v^{\prime})\neq 0
, then
U_{1}
is itself a
hyperbolic plane, so we have a splitting
V=U_{1}\oplus U_{1}^{\perp}
. The
orthogonal
U_{1}^{\perp}
has dimension
n-1\geq 4
and carries a quadratic
form of corank
\leq 2
, so it has an isotropic vector not contained in its
radical. This isotropic vector can be completed to a second hyperbolic plane

U_{2}
. In either case,
U\coloneqq U_{1}\oplus U_{2}\subset V
is a hyperbolic
subspace of dimension
4
containing
v
and
v^{\prime}
, as desired.

Finally, to conclude, since
q\rvert_{U}
is non-degenerate, there is an
orthogonal decomposition
V=U\oplus U^{\perp}
and a corresponding inclusion
of special Clifford groups

\mathbf{S\Gamma}(U,q\rvert_{U})\times_{\mathbf{k}}\mathbf{S\Gamma}(U^{\perp},q\rvert_{U^{\perp}})\subset\mathbf{S\Gamma}(V,q).

Since
U
is nonsingular, the morphism

\mathbf{S\Gamma}(U,q\rvert_{U})\to\mathbf{SO}(U,q\rvert_{U})
is surjective, as in

5.9
. Since
\dim_{\mathbf{k}}U\geq 2
, the special orthogonal
group acts transitively on its isotropic lines; this implies that there exists
an element
g^{\prime}\in\mathbf{S\Gamma}(U,q\rvert_{U})
taking

\mathbf{k}\cdot v
to
\mathbf{k}\cdot v^{\prime}
. Then the element

g\coloneqq(g^{\prime},1)\in\mathbf{S\Gamma}(V,q)
has the sought-after properties.
∎

5.11.
Hyperplane sections

—

The next few paragraphs describe the relationship between spinor sheaves on a
quadric and a hyperplane section when the hyperplane contains the defining
subspace. Let
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}
be a corank
1
subbundle
and let
q^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{L}
be the restriction of
q

thereon. The Clifford algebra of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
is then a graded subalgebra of the
Clifford algebra of
\mathcal{E}
, and the quotient can be identified as
follows: Writing
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
for the
quotient line bundle and
[-1]
for shift of grading, there is a short exact
sequence of graded
\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})
-modules:

0\to\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})\to\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})[-1]\to 0.

Proof.

Clifford multiplication gives a map

\mathcal{E}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})[-1]\to\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)/\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})

which vanishes on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})[-1]

and therefore factors through a map

\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})[-1]\to\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)/\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime}).

The degree
d
part of each side is a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module of
rank
2^{n-1}
and easy to see that a local basis on the left is sent
to one on the right, so the map is an isomorphism.
∎

More generally,
\mathcal{W}\subset\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
be an isotropic subbundle,
and write
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
for the associated Clifford
ideals in
\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)
and
\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})
, respectively.
Since both ideals are generated by
\det\mathcal{W}
,
\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
may be
identified as a sub-
\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})
-module of
\mathcal{I}
.
The argument above generalizes to yield:

5.12 Lemma
. —

In the setting of
5.11
, inclusion induces an
exact sequence graded of
\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})
-modules

0\to\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{I}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}[-1]\to 0.\qed

The sequence of Clifford ideals induces a corresponding sequence of spinor
sheaves. To set notation, write
\pi^{\prime}\colon\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\to S
and

\rho^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\to S
for the projective and quadric bundles associated
with
q^{\prime}\colon\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{L}
, and let
\mathcal{S}
and

\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
be the spinor sheaves corresponding to
\mathcal{W}
on

Q
and
Q^{\prime}
, respectively. Then:

5.13 Lemma
. —

In the setting of
5.11
, there is an exact
sequence

0\to\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{S}_{d}\rvert_{Q^{\prime}}\to\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathcal{S}_{d-1}^{\prime}\to 0.

Proof.

This can be deduced from
5.12
via the following
general construction: For any quasi-coherent graded

\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})
-module
\mathcal{M}
, Clifford multiplication
as in
5.3
provides a short exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{O}_{\pi^{\prime}}(-1)\otimes\pi^{\prime*}\mathcal{M}_{-1}\to\pi^{\prime*}\mathcal{M}_{0}\to\iota_{*}\mathcal{F}\to 0

where
\mathcal{F}
is a quasi-coherent sheaf on
Q^{\prime}
and

\iota\colon Q^{\prime}\to\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
is the inclusion, and the
assignment
\mathcal{M}\mapsto\mathcal{F}
determines an exact functor from
the category of quasi-coherent graded
\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})
-modules to the
category of quasi-coherent modules on
Q^{\prime}
.
∎

Going the other direction, the spinor sheaf
\mathcal{S}
on

Q
is related to the Clifford ideal
\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
via modification
by
\mathcal{S}^{\prime}
along the closed subscheme
j^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\to Q
. This
relation comes from considering the quotient of the Clifford ideal

\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}
by the span of the Clifford ideal
\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}^{\prime}

for the hyperplane section, and the twisted spinor sheaf

\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\otimes\mathcal{S}_{d-1}
identified as the image of
the Clifford multiplication map
\phi_{d}
from the first complex in

5.5
:

5.14 Proposition
. —

In the setting of
5.11
, assume additionally
that
j^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\to Q
is the inclusion of an effective Cartier divisor.
Then
\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{d}\cap\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\otimes\mathcal{S}_{d-1}=0

as submodules of
\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}
and

\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}/(\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{d}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\otimes\mathcal{S}_{d-1})\cong\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\otimes j^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{S}_{d-1}.

In particular, there is a short exact sequence

0\to\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\otimes\mathcal{S}_{d-1}\to\rho^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{d-1}^{\prime})\to\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\otimes j^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{S}^{\prime}_{d-1}\to 0.

Proof.

We first show that
\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\otimes\mathcal{S}_{d-1}
, viewed as the
image of

\phi_{d}\colon\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\otimes\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d-1}\to\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}

as above, intersects
\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}^{\prime}
trivially away on

Q\setminus Q^{\prime}
. There, the inclusion
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{E}

is split by the composite

\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\hookrightarrow\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}\to\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
,
and this induces a corresponding splitting of the sequence from

5.12
:

\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}|_{Q\setminus Q^{\prime}}\cong\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}\rvert_{Q\setminus Q^{\prime}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\otimes\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}[-1]\rvert_{Q\setminus Q^{\prime}}.

With respect to this splitting,
\phi_{d}\rvert_{Q\setminus Q^{\prime}}
may be identified as the map

(\varphi_{1},\varphi_{2})\colon\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\otimes\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d-1}^{\prime}\rvert_{Q\setminus Q^{\prime}}\oplus\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-2)\otimes\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d-2}^{\prime}\rvert_{Q\setminus Q^{\prime}}\longrightarrow\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}\rvert_{Q\setminus Q^{\prime}}

where
\varphi_{i}
is induced by
i
-fold Clifford multiplication. Now

\varphi_{2}=0
since it is multiplication by the equation of
Q
as in

5.3
, and
\varphi_{1}
is the inclusion of a direct
complement of
\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}^{\prime}\rvert_{Q\setminus Q^{\prime}}
since any local section
contains a local section in
\mathcal{E}\setminus\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
. This
shows that
\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}\cap\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\otimes\mathcal{S}_{d-1}

is zero upon restriction to
Q\setminus Q^{\prime}
. Since this is a
submodule of a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{Q}
-module, and the complement
Q\setminus Q^{\prime}

of an effective Cartier divisor is scheme-theoretically dense in
Q
,
in fact

\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}\cap\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\otimes\mathcal{S}_{d-1}=0
.

The quotient of
\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}
by the span of these two submodules may
be identified as the cokernel of

\psi\colon\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\otimes\mathcal{S}_{d-1}\to\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}\to\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}/\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}^{\prime}\cong\rho^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{d-1}^{\prime})

where the rightmost identification is from
5.12
.
We claim it is scheme-theoretically supported on
Q^{\prime}
. This is a
statement local on
S
, so assume that
\mathcal{L}\cong\mathcal{O}_{S}
and that

\mathcal{E}\cong\mathcal{O}_{S}\cdot e_{0}\oplus\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\cong\big(\bigoplus\nolimits_{i=0}^{n-r-1}\mathcal{O}_{S}\cdot e_{i}\big)\oplus\mathcal{W}\cong\bigoplus\nolimits_{i=0}^{n}\mathcal{O}_{S}\cdot e_{i}.

With these trivializations,

\rho^{*}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{d-1}^{\prime})\cong e_{0}\cdot\rho^{*}\mathit{C}\ell_{d-r-1}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})\cdot\det\mathcal{W}

and, writing
x_{i}
for the coordinates of
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E}\cong\mathbf{P}^{n}

dual to the basis
e_{i}
, the image of
\psi
may be identified with the
submodule

\big(x_{0}e_{0}\cdot\rho^{*}\mathit{C}\ell_{d-r-1}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})\cdot\det\mathcal{W}\big)+\big(e_{0}\cdot(x_{1}e_{1}+\cdots+x_{n}e_{n})\cdot\rho^{*}\mathit{C}\ell_{d-r-2}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})\cdot\det\mathcal{W}\big).

The first summand shows that
\coker\psi
is supported on the vanishing
locus
Q^{\prime}=Q\cap\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
of
x_{0}
.

Writing
\coker\psi\cong j^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{F}
, it remains to show that

\mathcal{F}\cong\rho^{\prime*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathcal{S}_{d-1}^{\prime}
. We have

\mathcal{F}=\coker j^{*}\psi=\coker\alpha
where
\alpha
is as the
diagonal map in

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{\prime*}\mathcal{I}_{d}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{\prime*}\mathcal{I}_{d}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho^{\prime*}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{d-1}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(-1)\otimes\rho^{\prime*}\mathcal{I}_{d-1}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(-1)\otimes\rho^{\prime*}\mathcal{I}_{d-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(-1)\otimes\rho^{\prime*}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathcal{I}_{d-2}^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{d}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{d}\rvert_{Q^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{d-1}^{\prime}}

where the rows are exact and the squares are commutative by

5.12
. We see that the cokernel of
\alpha
is equal to the cokernel of
\phi^{\prime}_{d-1}
which is equal to
\rho^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}\otimes j^{\prime}\mathcal{S}_{d-1}^{\prime}
.
∎

5.15.
Cones

—

The final few statements in this section describe the behaviour of spinor
sheaves along cones over quadrics. Let
\mathcal{K}\subset\mathcal{E}
be a
rank
c
subbundle contained in the radical of
q
, set

\bar{\mathcal{E}}\coloneqq\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{K}
, and let

\bar{q}\colon\bar{\mathcal{E}}\to\mathcal{L}
be the induced quadratic
form. The quotient map
\mathcal{E}\to\bar{\mathcal{E}}
induces a
short exact sequence of graded
\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)
-modules

0\to\mathcal{J}\to\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)\to\mathit{C}\ell(\bar{\mathcal{E}},\bar{q})\to 0

where the kernel is the sheaf of ideals generated by
\mathcal{K}
.
Since
\mathcal{K}
lies in the radical of
q
, it is central in

\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)
, and so
\mathcal{J}
is a two-sided ideal. The
quotient map endows each
\mathit{C}\ell(\bar{\mathcal{E}},\bar{q})
-module with the
structure of a
\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)
-module.

Let
\mathcal{W}\subset\mathcal{E}
be an isotropic subbundle of rank

r
containing
\mathcal{K}
, and let

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{W}}\subset\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{E}}
be the corresponding
isotropic bundle in the quotient. The Clifford ideals
\mathcal{I}
and

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{I}}
associated with
\mathcal{W}
and

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{W}}
, respectively, are related as follows:

5.16 Lemma
. —

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{I}}_{d}\cong\mathcal{I}_{d+c}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}\det\mathcal{K}^{\vee}

as
\mathit{C}\ell_{0}(\mathcal{E},q)
-modules
for each
d\in\mathbf{Z}
.

Proof.

The annihilator in
\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)
of the generator

\det\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{W}}
of
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{I}}
is the preimage of
the sheaf of left ideals in
\mathit{C}\ell(\bar{\mathcal{E}},\bar{q})
generated by

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{W}}
. Since

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{W}}=\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{K}
, and
\mathcal{K}

is central in
\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)
, this is the sheaf of left ideals in

\mathit{C}\ell(\mathcal{E},q)
generated by
\mathcal{W}
. So Clifford multiplication
gives rise to a presentation

\mathit{C}\ell_{d-r+c-1}(\mathcal{E},q)\otimes\mathcal{W}\otimes\det\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{W}}\to\mathit{C}\ell_{d-r+c}(\mathcal{E},q)\otimes\det\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{W}}\to\bar{\mathcal{I}}_{d}\to 0.

Since

\det\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{W}}\cong\det\mathcal{W}\otimes\det\mathcal{K}^{\vee}
,
this is also the presentation for

\mathcal{I}_{d+c}\otimes\det\mathcal{K}^{\vee}
.
∎

As usual, linear projection centred along
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{K}
results in a
commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{P}\bar{\mathcal{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b_{Q}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{\bar{Q}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\iota}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\bar{\rho}}

where
b\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{E}
and

b_{Q}\colon\widetilde{Q}\to Q
are the blowups of
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E}
and

Q
along
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{K}
, respectively, and

a\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{E}\to\mathbf{P}\bar{\mathcal{E}}
is the morphism
resolving linear projection. The blowup
\widetilde{Q}
may be identified as
the restriction of linear projection

a\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{E}\to\mathbf{P}\bar{\mathcal{E}}
over the base
of the cone
\bar{Q}
; in other words, the top right square in the diagram is
Cartesian. The spinor sheaves
\mathcal{S}
and
\bar{\mathcal{S}}

associated with
\mathcal{W}
and
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{W}}
, respectively,
are related as follows:

5.17 Proposition
. —

Rb_{Q,*}La_{\bar{Q}}^{*}\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{d}\cong\mathcal{S}_{d+c}\otimes\rho^{*}\det\mathcal{K}^{\vee}

in
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(Q)
for each
d\in\mathbf{Z}
.

Proof.

View the defining presentation of
\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{d}
on

\mathbf{P}\bar{\mathcal{E}}
as an exact triangle in
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathbf{P}\bar{\mathcal{E}})

of the form

\mathcal{O}_{\bar{\pi}}(-1)\otimes\bar{\pi}^{*}\bar{\mathcal{I}}_{d-1}\longrightarrow\bar{\pi}^{*}\bar{\mathcal{I}}_{d}\longrightarrow R\bar{\iota}_{*}\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}

Apply
Rb_{*}La^{*}
to obtain a triangle in
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E})
, the first
two terms of which are easy to identify: Commutativity of the
diagram, the projection formula, and
5.16
together give
the first line of

\displaystyle Rb_{*}La^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\bar{\pi}}(-i)\otimes\bar{\pi}^{*}\bar{\mathcal{I}}_{d-i})

\displaystyle\cong Rb_{*}a^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\bar{\pi}}(-i)\otimes\pi^{*}(\mathcal{I}_{d+c-i}\otimes\det\mathcal{K}^{\vee})

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{O}_{\pi}(-i)\otimes\pi^{*}(\mathcal{I}_{d+c-i}\otimes\det\mathcal{K}^{\vee})\;\text{for $i=0,1$}.

For the second line, note that

a^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\bar{\pi}}(-1)\cong\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{E}}(E)\otimes b^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\pi}(-1)

where
E\subset\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{E}
is the exceptional divisor, and
so
Rb_{*}a^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\bar{\pi}}(-i)\cong\mathcal{O}_{\pi}(-i)
for
i=0,1
. As for
the third term in the new triangle, observe that

a\colon\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{E}\to\mathbf{P}\bar{\mathcal{E}}
is a
projective bundle, so it is tor independent with

\bar{\iota}\colon\bar{Q}\to\mathbf{P}\bar{\mathcal{E}}
, and thus

La^{*}\circ R\bar{\iota}_{*}=R\tilde{\iota}\circ La_{\bar{Q}}^{*}
as functors

\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\bar{Q})\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widetilde{\mathbf{P}}\mathcal{E})
by

[
Stacks
,
0E23
]
. Commutativity of the top left square above then shows that

Rb_{*}\circ R\tilde{\iota}=R\iota_{*}\circ Rb_{Q,*}
. Therefore the new
triangle is

\mathcal{O}_{\pi}(-1)\otimes\pi^{*}(\mathcal{I}_{d+c-1}\otimes\det\mathcal{K}^{\vee})\longrightarrow\pi^{*}(\mathcal{I}_{d+c}\otimes\det\mathcal{K}^{\vee})\longrightarrow R\iota_{*}Rb_{Q,*}La_{\bar{Q}}^{*}\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{d}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}

Comparing with the defining sequence for
\mathcal{S}_{d+c}
now shows that

R\iota_{*}Rb_{Q,*}L_{\bar{Q}}^{*}\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{d}\cong R\iota_{*}(\mathcal{S}_{d+c}\otimes\rho^{*}\det\mathcal{K}^{\vee})\in\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E}).

Since the closed immersion
\iota\colon Q\to\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E}
is, in
particular, affine, this at least shows that

Rb_{Q,*}L_{\bar{Q}}^{*}\bar{\mathcal{S}}_{d}
is a complex concentrated in degree

0
. Using that
\iota_{*}
is fully faithful on quasi-coherent modules, see

[
Stacks
,
01QY
]
, the sheaf in degree
0
is identified
with
\mathcal{S}_{d+c}\otimes\rho^{*}\deg\mathcal{K}^{\vee}
,
whence the result.
∎

6.
Kuznetsov components under hyperbolic reduction

Returning to the setting of
4.14
, suppose
the quadric bundle
\rho\colon Q\to S
admits a regular section
corresponding to a rank
1
subbundle
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{E}
,
and let
\rho^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\to S
be the associated hyperbolic reduction. Assume
throughout that
S_{n-1}
does not contain any weakly associated points of

S
, so that
Q^{\prime}
is an effective Cartier divisor in

\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{F}^{\perp}/\mathcal{F})
by

4.19
. The aim in the first half of this
section is to relate the Kuznetsov components of
Q
and
Q^{\prime}
.
Specifically, a mutations argument gives in
6.1
an

S
-linear equivalence
\Phi\colon\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
. We are then able
to explicitly identify the kernel underlying
\Phi
in

6.2
, with which we show in

6.3
that

\Phi
sends the dual spinor sheaves of
Q^{\prime}
to those of
Q
.

To begin, continue with the notation from the diagram in

4.15
. By its construction together with

4.18
, the scheme

\widetilde{\rho}\colon\widetilde{Q}\to S
is a blowup in two ways, and fits
into a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{P}\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\imath}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\jmath}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\prime}}

of schemes over
S
. Orlov’s blowup formula from

[
Orl92
, Theorem 4.3]
, see also
[
Kuz14
, Theorem 1.6]
,
therefore gives two semiorthogonal decompositions of

\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widetilde{Q})
: On the one hand, viewing
\widetilde{Q}
as a
blowup of
Q
along
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{F}
yields a
S
-linear semiorthogonal
decomposition of
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widetilde{Q})
of the form

(1)

\big\langle Lb^{*}\mathrm{Ku}(Q),\,L\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S)\otimes^{L}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}},\,\ldots,\,L\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S)\otimes^{L}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}((n-2)H),\\
L\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S)\otimes^{L}R\tilde{\imath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{E},\,\ldots,\,L\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S)\otimes^{L}R\tilde{\imath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{E}(-(n-3)E)\big\rangle

where
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(H)\coloneqq b^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(1)
. On the other hand,
viewing
\widetilde{Q}
as a blowup of
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
along

Q^{\prime}
and writing
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(h)\coloneqq a^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1)

yields the
S
-linear semiorthogonal decomposition

(2)

\big\langle R\tilde{\jmath}_{*}(La_{\Lambda}^{*}\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime})\otimes^{L}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda)),\,L\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S)\otimes^{L}R\tilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda),\,\ldots,\,L\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S)\otimes^{L}R\tilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda+(n-4)h),\\
L\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S)\otimes^{L}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}},\,\ldots,\,L\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S)\otimes^{L}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}((n-1)h)\big\rangle.

The following matches the two Kuznetsov components via a series of mutations:

6.1 Proposition
. —

As
S
-linear subcategories of
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widetilde{Q})
,

Lb^{*}\mathrm{Ku}(Q)=\mathbf{L}_{L\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S)\otimes^{L}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(-E)}\big(R\tilde{\jmath}_{*}(La_{\Lambda}^{*}\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime})\otimes^{L}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda))\big).

This determines an
S
-linear equivalence of categories

\Phi\colon\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
.

Compare the proof below to the proof of
[
Xie23
, Theorem 4.2]
.

Proof.

Starting with the semiorthogonal decomposition (
1
), we
perform a series of mutations, which are justified by

3.4
, to arrive at a decomposition which may be
compared with (
2
). The overall strategy is to carefully move
the components supported on
E
completely toward the left, so that they end
up adjacent to the Kuznetsov component of
Q
. In what follows, all
functors are derived, so we suppress the
L
and
R
for left and right
derived functors. Moreover, all constructions are linear over
S
, so to ease
the notation, we suppress the tensor over
L\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S)

and simply denote by an exceptional object the corresponding
S
-linear
subcategory it generates.

Step 1.

Starting from
\tilde{\imath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{E}
and moving right, for each
k=0,\ldots,n-3
, move
\tilde{\imath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{E}(-kE)
to the left via
n-2-k

right mutations. Each mutation is of the form

\langle\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(mH-kE),\,\tilde{\imath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{E}(-kE)\rangle=\langle\tilde{\imath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{E}(-kE),\,\mathbf{R}_{\tilde{\imath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{E}(-kE)}(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(mH-(k+1)E))\rangle.

To determine the right mutation, note
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(H)

restricts trivially to
E
because
\widetilde{Q}\to Q
is a blowup along
a section, so that

\rho_{*}b_{*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(mH-kE),\tilde{\imath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{E}(-kE))\cong\rho_{*}b_{*}\tilde{\imath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{E}\cong\mathcal{O}_{S},

generated by the equation of
E
in
\widetilde{Q}
. Therefore the
right mutation of
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(mH-kE)
, which is the cone of
the coevaluation map above up to a twist, is

\operatorname{cone}(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}\to\tilde{\imath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{E})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(mH-kE)\cong\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(mH-(k+1)E).

This series of right mutations produces a semiorthogonal decomposition

\langle b^{*}\mathrm{Ku}(Q),\mathcal{A}_{0},\ldots,\mathcal{A}_{n-3},\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}((n-2)h)\rangle

where

\mathcal{A}_{k}=\langle\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(kH-kE),\tilde{\imath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{E}(-kE)\rangle

for each
k=0,\ldots,n-3
.

Step 2.
Within each of the blocks
\mathcal{A}_{k}
, left mutate

\tilde{\imath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{E}(-kE)
through
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(kH-kE)
. The
computation from step 1 implies that this produces decompositions

\mathcal{A}_{k}=\langle\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(kH-(k+1)E),\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(kH-kE)\rangle=\langle\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\Lambda+(k-1)h),\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(kh)\rangle,

for
k=0,\ldots,n-3
, where the second equality arises from the relations

\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(H-E)\cong\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(h)\;\;\text{and}\;\;\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(H-2E)\cong\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\Lambda)\otimes\widetilde{\rho}^{*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\vee})

from
4.16
(iii)

and
4.18
, respectively, and the fact that twisting by

\widetilde{\rho}^{*}(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\vee})
does not affect
the
S
-linear subcategory an object generates. Regroup the resulting
decomposition of
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widetilde{Q})
as follows:

\langle b^{*}\mathrm{Ku}(Q),\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(-E),\mathcal{B}_{0},\ldots,\mathcal{B}_{n-4},\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}((n-3)h),\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}((n-2)h)\rangle

where

\mathcal{B}_{k}\coloneqq\langle\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(kh),\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\Lambda+kh)\rangle

for
k=0,\ldots,n-4
.

Step 3.
Within each of the blocks
\mathcal{B}_{k}
, left mutate

\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\Lambda+kh)
through
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(kh)
.
Since
\Lambda
is the exceptional divisor of the blowup
a
,

\widetilde{\rho}_{*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(kh),\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\Lambda+kh))\cong\widetilde{\rho}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\Lambda)\cong\rho^{\prime}_{*}a_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\Lambda)\cong\mathcal{O}_{S},

the generator being an equation of
\Lambda
. Therefore the left mutation of

\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\Lambda+kh)
, being the cone of the evaluation map, is

\operatorname{cone}(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\Lambda))\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(kh)\cong\tilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda+kh).

This gives

\mathcal{B}_{k}=\langle\tilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda+kh),\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(kh)\rangle

for
k=0,\ldots,n-4
.

Step 4.
Since
\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda)
restricts to

\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}^{1}}(-1)
along the fibres of
\Lambda\to Q^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\rho}_{*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\widetilde{Q}}(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(kh),\tilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda+\ell h))

\displaystyle\cong\rho^{\prime}_{*}a_{*}\tilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda+(\ell-k)h)

\displaystyle\cong\rho^{\prime}_{*}j^{\prime}_{*}a_{\Lambda,*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda+(\ell-k)h)=0.

Therefore the
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(kh)
are totally orthogonal to the

\tilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda+\ell h)
for all integers
k
and

\ell
, and so the decomposition can be reordered to

\langle b^{*}\mathrm{Ku}(Q),\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(-E),\tilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda),\ldots,\tilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda+(n-4)h),\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}},\ldots,\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}((n-2)h)\rangle.

Step 5.
Right mutate
b^{*}\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
through
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(-E)

and apply Serre duality for the morphism

\widetilde{\rho}\colon\widetilde{Q}\to S
to move

\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(-E)
to the right side. Since

\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(-E)\otimes\omega_{\widetilde{\rho}}^{\vee}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}((n-1)h)\otimes\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathcal{M}

for some invertible
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-module
\mathcal{M}
, this yields the

S
-linear semiorthgonal decomposition

\langle\mathbf{R}_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(-E)}(b^{*}\mathrm{Ku}(Q)),\tilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda),\ldots,\tilde{\jmath}_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda+(n-4)h),\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}},\ldots,\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}((n-1)h)\rangle.

Comparing with the semiorthogonal decomposition (
2
) shows
that

\mathbf{R}_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(-E)}(b^{*}\mathrm{Ku}(Q))=\tilde{\jmath}_{*}(a_{\Lambda}^{*}\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}(\Lambda))

as
S
-linear subcategories of
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(\widetilde{Q})
. Applying left
mutation through
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(-E)
on both sides then completes
the proof.
∎

Since
\Phi\colon\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
is a composition of pullbacks,
pushforwards, tensor products, and mutations, it is of Fourier–Mukai type. To
determine a kernel, consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{Q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q\times_{S}\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q\times_{S}Q^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\jmath}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{2}}

where
j\colon\Lambda\to Q\times_{S}Q^{\prime}
is the closed immersion as the
point-line incidence correspondence, resulting from the geometric interpretation
of
\rho^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\to S
from
4.11
as
the relative scheme of lines in
\rho\colon Q\to S
incident with the
section
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{F}
:

\Lambda=\big\{(x,[\ell])\in Q\times_{S}Q^{\prime}:x\in\ell,\;\ell\subset Q\;\text{a line through the section}\;\mathbf{P}\mathcal{F}\big\}.

Let
\mathcal{J}
be the ideal sheaf of
\Lambda
in
Q\times_{S}Q^{\prime}
.
The result is as follows:

6.2 Proposition
. —

\Phi\colon\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
is the Fourier–Mukai functor with kernel

\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\big(\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1)\otimes\rho^{\prime*}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L})\big)\otimes\mathcal{J}\in\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(Q\times_{S}Q^{\prime}).

Proof.

Functors in this proof are all derived. By construction,
\Phi
sends an
object
F\in\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime})
to
M\in\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
characterized by

\displaystyle Lb^{*}M

\displaystyle\cong\operatorname{cone}\big(\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathcal{H}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(-E)\to\tilde{\jmath}_{*}(a_{\Lambda}^{*}F\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda/\widetilde{Q}})\big)

\displaystyle\cong\operatorname{cone}\big(\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathcal{H}\to\tilde{\jmath}_{*}a_{\Lambda}^{*}(F\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1))\otimes\widetilde{\rho}^{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L})\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(-E),

where
\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda/\widetilde{\mathcal{Q}}}
is identified as

\tilde{\jmath}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(h-E)\otimes\widetilde{\rho}^{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L}))

via
4.16
(iii)
and

4.18
, and
\mathcal{H}
is the object

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}

\displaystyle\coloneqq\widetilde{\rho}_{*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(-E),\tilde{\jmath}_{*}(a^{*}F\otimes\mathcal{N}_{\Lambda/\widetilde{Q}}))

\displaystyle\>\cong\widetilde{\rho}_{*}\tilde{\jmath}_{*}a^{*}(F\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1))\otimes(\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L})\cong\rho^{\prime}_{*}(F\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1))\otimes(\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L})\in\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S).

Since
b\colon\widetilde{Q}\to Q
is a blowup along a regular section,

b_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(E)=\mathcal{O}_{Q}
, so the projection formula gives an isomorphism
M\cong b_{*}(b^{*}M\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Q}}(E))
.
Therefore there is a canonical isomorphism

\Phi(F)\cong b_{*}\operatorname{cone}\big(\widetilde{\rho}^{*}\mathcal{H}\to\tilde{\jmath}_{*}a^{*}(F\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1))\otimes\widetilde{\rho}^{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L})\big).

Writing

\rho^{*}\mathcal{H}=\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(F\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1))\otimes\rho^{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L})

and
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\colon Q\times_{S}Q^{\prime}\to Q
for the first projection, the
relations
a=\mathrm{pr}_{2}\circ j
and
b\circ\tilde{\jmath}=\mathrm{pr}_{1}\circ j

from the diagram above simplifies this to

\displaystyle\Phi(F)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}\operatorname{cone}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(F\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1))\to j_{*}j^{*}\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(F\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1))\big)\otimes\rho^{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L})

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}\operatorname{cone}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(F\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1))\to\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(F\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1))\otimes j_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}\big)\otimes\rho^{*}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L})

where the map is restriction to
\Lambda
. Thus the cone is

\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(F\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1))
tensored with the ideal sheaf

\mathcal{J}
of
\Lambda
in
Q\times_{S}Q^{\prime}
, and so

\Phi(F)\cong\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(F)\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\big(\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1)\otimes\rho^{\prime*}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L})\big)\otimes\mathcal{J}).\qed

A particularly useful feature of the equivalence

\Phi\colon\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
is that it is compatible with the
construction of spinor sheaves. More precisely, let

\mathcal{W}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{F}^{\perp}/\mathcal{F}
be an isotropic
subbundle, and let
\mathcal{W}
be the corresponding subbundle of in

\mathcal{E}
. For each integer
d
, these define spinor sheaves

\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{S}_{d}
on
Q^{\prime}
and
Q
, respectively.
By
5.6
, these may be viewed as objects in

\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime})
and
\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
, respectively. The statement is that
\Phi

sends the dual of one to the dual of the other:

6.3 Proposition
. —

\Phi(\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime\vee})\cong\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee}
for all
d\in\mathbf{Z}
.

Proof.

All functors are again derived in this proof. Continuing with the notation from
the diagram preceding
6.2
, tensor the
ideal sheaf sequence for
j\colon\Lambda\to Q\times_{S}Q^{\prime}
with

\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime}
and pushforward along

\mathrm{pr}_{1}\colon Q\times_{S}Q^{\prime}\to Q
to obtain the triangle

\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{J})\longrightarrow\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime}\longrightarrow(\mathrm{pr}_{1}\circ j)_{*}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}\circ j)^{*}\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}

in
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(Q)
. The defining presentation for the spinor sheaf from

5.3
identifies the middle term as

\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime}\cong\rho^{*}\rho_{*}^{\prime}\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime}\cong\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d}^{\prime}

where
\mathcal{I}_{d}
is the
d
-th Clifford ideal on
S
associated with

\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
. To identify the term on the right, let
Q^{\prime\prime}
be the
intersection of
Q
with its tangent space along
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{F}
; in other
words, let
q^{\prime\prime}\colon\mathcal{F}^{\perp}\to\mathcal{L}
be the
restriction of
q
to the orthogonal of
\mathcal{F}
, and let
Q^{\prime\prime}

be the associated quadric bundle in
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{F}^{\perp}
. Then
Q^{\prime\prime}

is a cone over
Q^{\prime}
with vertex
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{F}
, and the morphism

\mathrm{pr}_{1}\circ j\colon\Lambda\to Q
factors through a morphism

b_{\Lambda}\colon\Lambda\to Q^{\prime\prime}
identifying it with the blowup along
the vertex. Thus there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Lambda}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q\times_{S}Q^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b_{\Lambda}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{pr}_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime\prime}}

Writing
a_{\Lambda}=\mathrm{pr}_{2}\circ j\colon\Lambda\to Q^{\prime}
, the term on the
right is identified via
5.17
as the pushforward to

Q
of a spinor sheaf on
Q^{\prime\prime}
associated with
\mathcal{W}
:

(\mathrm{pr}_{1}\circ j)_{*}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}\circ j)^{*}\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime}\cong i^{\prime\prime}_{*}b_{\Lambda,*}a_{\Lambda}^{*}\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime}\cong i^{\prime\prime}_{*}\mathcal{S}_{d+1}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\rho^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\vee}.

Identifying furthermore the Clifford ideal
\mathcal{I}_{d}^{\prime}
for

\rho^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\to S
with the twisted Clifford ideal

\mathcal{I}_{d+1}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}
for

\rho^{\prime\prime}\colon Q^{\prime\prime}\to S
via
5.16
transforms the
triangle to

\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{J})\longrightarrow\rho^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d+1}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\rho^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\longrightarrow i^{\prime\prime}_{*}\mathcal{S}_{d+1}^{\prime\prime}\otimes\rho^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}

where the second map is induced by the evaluation map. Comparing with

5.14
and using the fact that

\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{F}^{\perp}\cong\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L}

shows that

(
\star
)

\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{J})\cong\mathcal{S}_{d+1}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\otimes\rho^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\vee}.

To conclude, it remains to compare the two spinor sheaves to their duals via
the relations in
5.3
. Writing the rank of
\mathcal{E}
as

2k
or
2k+1
, and that of
\mathcal{W}
as
r
, the relations give

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(-1)

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{S}_{r-d-2}^{\prime\vee}\otimes\rho^{\prime*}(\det\mathcal{W}^{\prime}\otimes\det(\mathcal{F}^{\perp}/\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\vee,\otimes k-1}),\;\text{and}

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{d+1}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\rho\phantom{{}^{\prime}}}(-1)

\displaystyle\cong\mathcal{S}_{r-d-2}^{\vee}\otimes\rho^{*}(\det\mathcal{W}\otimes\det\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\vee,\otimes k}).

Since
\det\mathcal{W}^{\prime}\cong\det\mathcal{W}\otimes\mathcal{F}^{\vee}

and
\det(\mathcal{F}^{\perp}/\mathcal{F})\cong\det\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{L}^{\vee}
,
substituting these identifications into
(

⋆

‣
6
), cancelling out common
invertible factors, and multiplying through by
\rho^{*}\mathcal{L}
shows

\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime\vee}\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\big(\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1)\otimes\rho^{\prime*}(\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L})\big)\otimes\mathcal{J}\big)\cong\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee}.

The functor on the left is
\Phi
by

6.2
, yielding the result.
∎

One simple application of
6.3
is
to show that spinor sheaves are relatively simple in certain situations.
Note that this is not always the case: see
[
Add11
, Proposition
6.2]
.

6.4 Corollary
. —

Let
\rho\colon Q\to S
be a quadric
2\ell
-fold bundle,

\mathbf{P}\mathcal{W}\to S
an isotropic
\ell
-plane intersecting

\Sing\rho
in at most
1
point in each fibre, and

\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee}
the associated spinor sheaf. Then the canonical map

\mathcal{O}_{S}\to\rho_{*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee},\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee})

is an isomorphism for any
d\in\mathbf{Z}
. In other words,

\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee}
is simple over
S
.

Proof.

The assertion is local on
S
. Passing to an étale cover, we may assume
that
\mathcal{W}
admits a line subbundle
\mathcal{N}
. Let

\rho^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\to S
be the associated hyperbolic reduction, set

\mathcal{W}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{W}/\mathcal{N}
, and write

\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime\vee}
for the corresponding
d
-th dual spinor sheaf.
There is an
S
-linear equivalence
\Phi\colon\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Ku}(Q)

satisfying
\mathcal{S}^{\vee}_{d}\cong\Phi(\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime\vee})
by

6.1
and
6.3
, so
this induces an isomorphism

R\rho_{*}R\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{S}^{\vee}_{d},\mathcal{S}^{\vee}_{d})\cong R\rho^{\prime}_{*}R\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q^{\prime}}}(\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime\vee},\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime\vee}).

By induction, then, it suffices to treat the case when
\rho\colon Q\to S

has relative dimension
0
and
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{W}\to S
provides a section

w\colon S\to Q
of
\rho
. In this case,

5.7
shows that the spinor sheaves

\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee}
are, up to invertible factors, of the form

i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T}
for a closed subscheme
i\colon T\to Q
cut out in the

\mathbf{P}^{1}
-bundle
\pi\colon\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E}\to S
by a line bundle of
relative degree
1
. Noting that
\rho
is flat and
i
is affine, taking
degree
0
cohomology sheaves then gives the result:

\rho_{*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee},\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee})\cong\rho_{*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T},i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T})\cong\rho_{*}i_{*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{T}}(\mathcal{O}_{T},\mathcal{O}_{T})\cong\rho_{*}i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{T}\cong\mathcal{O}_{S}.\qed

6.5.
Maximal hyperbolic reduction

—

Let
\rho\colon Q\to S
be a quadric bundle of even relative dimension

2\ell
. Suppose that there exists a regular isotropic subbundle

\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{E}
of rank
\ell
; in particular, this
implies that
S_{3}=\varnothing
. Assuming furthermore that
S_{2}

contains no weakly associated points of
S
, the corresponding hyperbolic
reduction
\mu\colon M\to S
is a family of quadrics of relative dimension

0
, having
1
-dimensional fibres over points of
S_{2}
. As such, we
refer to
\mu\colon M\to S
as a
maximal hyperbolic reduction
of the
quadric bundle
\rho\colon Q\to S
.

Identify
M
via
4.11
as the subscheme of
the relative Fano scheme
\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)
parameterizing

\ell
-planes along
\rho\colon Q\to S
that contain
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{F}
,
and let
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
be the tautological isotropic subbundle of
rank
\ell+1
in
\mu^{*}\mathcal{E}
. In this way, the quadric bundle

Q\times_{S}M\subset\mathbf{P}\mathcal{E}\times_{S}M\cong\mathbf{P}(\mu^{*}\mathcal{E})

over
M
via
\mathrm{pr}_{2}\colon Q\times_{S}M\to M
carries a tautological
family
\mathbf{P}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}
of isotropic
\ell
-planes. Writing

\mathcal{S}_{d}
for the associated degree
d
spinor sheaf on

Q\times_{S}M
, we have the following:

6.6 Proposition
. —

In the setting above, the Fourier–Mukai functor

\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(M)\to\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(Q)
given by

{}_{d}(F)\coloneqq R\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}(L\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}F\otimes^{L}\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee})

factors through
\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
and defines an
S
-linear equivalence

{}_{d}\colon\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(M)\to\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
for each
d\in\mathbf{Z}
.

Proof.

To ease notation, all functors in this proof are derived.
That the functor factors through
\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
follows from

5.6
: Indeed, using

\rho\circ\mathrm{pr}_{1}=\mu\circ\mathrm{pr}_{2}
, the fact that the relative tautological
bundle of
\mathrm{pr}_{2}\colon Q\times_{S}M\to M
is

\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(1)\coloneqq\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(1)
, and the projection formula,

\displaystyle\rho_{*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(i),{}_{d}(F))

\displaystyle\cong\rho_{*}\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}F\otimes\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q\times_{S}M}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(i),\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee}))

\displaystyle\cong\mu_{*}(F\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{2,*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q\times_{S}M}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(i),\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee}))

\displaystyle=0\;\;\text{for}\;0\leq i\leq 2r-1.

Henceforth, view
d
as a functor
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(M)\to\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
.
Show that this is an equivalence via induction on the integer
\ell\geq 1
.
When
\ell=1
, the even degree
d=2k
dual spinors

\mathcal{S}_{2k}^{\vee}
are by
5.8
,
up to twists by invertible objects pulled back from
S
, the ideal sheaf

\mathcal{J}
of the tautological line

j\colon\Lambda\hookrightarrow Q\times_{S}M
. In this case,

6.1
and
6.2

together imply that
2k
is an equivalence. For odd
d=2k+1
,

5.8
shows that, up to twists by
invertible objects pulled up from
S
,
2k+1
is of the form

F\mapsto\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}F\otimes\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q\times_{S}M}}(\mathcal{J},\mathcal{O}_{Q\times_{S}M})\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\rho}(-1)\big).

Since the ideal sheaf
\mathcal{J}
is pseudo-coherent,
\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}F
may
be brought into the
\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}
-complex; since
\mu\colon M\to S
is an
effective Cartier divisor of relative degree
2
in a
\mathbf{P}^{1}
-bundle over

S
, it has a dualizing sheaf which is the pullback of a line bundle on

S
. Therefore, the functor above is, up to invertible objects from
S
,
isomorphic to

F\mapsto\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{1,*}(\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{M}}(F,\mathcal{O}_{M})\otimes\mathcal{J}),\mathcal{O}_{Q}(-1)\big).

This shows that
2k+1
is obtained from the Fourier–Mukai equivalence
of
6.1
and
6.2

upon applying autoequivalences to the source and target, and so
2k+1

itself is an equivalence. This establishes the base case of the induction.

Assume
\ell\geq 2
. By
1.23
, which applies thanks
to
6.7
, it suffices to show that
d
is an
equivalence after replacing
S
by an étale cover.
Doing so, assume further that
\mathcal{F}
admits a line subbundle

\mathcal{N}
, and let
\rho^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\to S
be the associated hyperbolic
reduction. Then
\mu\colon M\to S
is the maximal hyperbolic reduction of
the quadric
(2\ell-2)
-fold bundle
\rho^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\to S

along the regular isotropic
(\ell-1)
-bundle

\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\coloneqq\mathcal{F}/\mathcal{N}
. The constructions of

6.5
then apply to yield, for each integer
d
,
dual spinor sheaves

\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime\vee}\;\text{on the quadric bundle}\;\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\times_{S}M\to M

constructed from the tautological isotropic rank
\ell
subbundle

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}
of
\mu^{*}(\mathcal{N}^{\perp}/\mathcal{N})
.
The inductive hypothesis implies that the corresponding Fourier–Mukai functor

{}_{d}^{\prime}\colon\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(M)\to\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime})
is an equivalence. Let

\Phi\colon\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
be the equivalence from

6.1
. We now show that
d
and

\Phi\circ{}_{d}^{\prime}
are isomorphic as functors
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(M)\to\mathrm{Ku}(Q)
,
from which the result follows.

On the one hand,
\Phi\circ{}_{d}^{\prime}
is a Fourier–Mukai functor whose
kernel, in view of
6.2
, is

\mathcal{K}\coloneqq\mathrm{pr}_{1,3,*}\big(\mathrm{pr}_{1,2}^{*}\mathcal{J}\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{O}_{\rho^{\prime}}(1)\otimes\rho^{\prime*}(\mathcal{N}^{\vee}\otimes\mathcal{L}))\otimes\mathrm{pr}_{2,3}^{*}\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime\vee}\big)\in\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(Q\times_{S}M),

where
\mathcal{J}
is the ideal sheaf of the incidence correspondence

\Lambda\hookrightarrow Q\times_{S}Q^{\prime}
as before. On the other hand,
base change along
\mu\colon M\to S
, and view the
quadric bundle
\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\times_{S}M\to M
as the hyperbolic
reduction of
\mathrm{pr}_{2}\colon Q\times_{S}M\to M
along the regular isotropic
line subbundle
\mu^{*}\mathcal{N}\subset\mu^{*}\mathcal{E}
. Applying

6.1
to this situation thus gives an equivalence

\Phi_{M}\colon\mathrm{Ku}(Q^{\prime}\times_{S}M)\to\mathrm{Ku}(Q\times_{S}M).

The tautological incidence correspondence between
Q\times_{S}M
and

Q^{\prime}\times M
is none other than

\Lambda\times_{S}M=\mathrm{pr}_{1,2}^{-1}(\Lambda)\hookrightarrow Q\times_{S}Q^{\prime}\times_{S}M\cong(Q\times_{S}M)\times_{M}(Q^{\prime}\times_{S}M),

so its ideal sheaf is
\mathrm{pr}_{1,2}^{*}\mathcal{J}
.
Comparing with
6.2
then shows that

\mathcal{K}\cong\Phi_{M}(\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime\vee})\in\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(Q\times_{S}M).

Since
\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\prime\vee}
is the
d
-th dual spinor bundle associated
with
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}=\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}/\mathcal{N}
on

Q^{\prime}\times_{S}M
, and
\Phi_{M}
respects dual spinor sheaves by

6.3
, it follows that

\mathcal{K}\cong\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee}
. Thus
\Phi\circ{}_{d}^{\prime}

and
d
are both Fourier–Mukai transforms
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(M)\to\mathrm{Ku}(Q)

with isomorphic kernels, and so the functors are isomorphic.
∎

In order to apply
1.23
, we need to verify that the
tautological dual spinor sheaves
\mathcal{S}_{d}^{\vee}
are relatively
perfect relative to both factors of
Q\times_{S}M
. That it is
M
-perfect
follows from
5.4
, whereas
Q
-perfectness can
be established by induction using the technique of

6.3
:

6.7 Lemma
. —

In the setting of
6.5
, the dual spinor sheaf

\mathcal{S}^{\vee}_{d}
is perfect relative to
Q
.

Proof.

Induct on the integer
\ell\geq 1
. The base case
\ell=1
concerns
a quadric surface bundle
\rho\colon Q\to S
and by

5.8
and

6.2
, ignoring twists by line bundles, the
ideal sheaf
\mathcal{J}
of the tautological line

j\colon\Lambda\to Q\times_{S}M
. By the ideal sheaf sequence, it suffices
to verify that
\mathcal{O}_{Q\times_{S}M}
and
j_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}
are
Q
-perfect.
For the former, observe that first projection factors as

\mathrm{pr}_{1}\colon Q\times_{S}M\hookrightarrow Q\times_{S}\mathbf{P}(\mathcal{E}/\mathcal{F})\to Q

where the first arrow is a closed immersion, which is regular by the standing
assumption on weakly associated points together with

4.19
, and the second arrow is a projective
bundle. Thus
\mathrm{pr}_{1}
is a composition of perfect morphisms, and thus is itself
perfect, meaning that
\mathcal{O}_{Q\times_{S}M}
is
Q
-perfect. For

j_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}
, as explained above
6.2
,
the map
\Lambda\to Q
factors as
b\circ\tilde{\jmath}
where

\tilde{\jmath}
is the inclusion of an effective Cartier divisor and
b

is a blowup in a local complete intersection closed subscheme. Therefore

\Lambda\to Q
is an local complete intersection morphism in the sense of

[
Stacks
,
068E
]
, and so
j_{*}\mathcal{O}_{\Lambda}
is perfect relative to
Q
by

[
Stacks
,
069H
]
. This establishes the base case.

Assume
\ell>1
and that the tautological dual spinors between a quadric

2(\ell-1)
-bundle and its maximal hyperbolic reduction is perfect relative
to the quadric. Let
\rho\colon Q\to S
be a quadric
2\ell
-fold bundle
with maximal hyperbolic reduction
\mu\colon M\to S
with respect to a
regular isotropic subbundle
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{E}
of rank
\ell
.
The problem is local on
S
, so pass to an étale cover to assume that

\mathcal{F}
admits a line subbundle
\mathcal{N}
so that the associated
hyperbolic reduction
\rho^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\to S
shares
\mu\colon M\to S
as
its maximal hyperbolic reduction along
\mathcal{F}/\mathcal{N}
. The proof
of
6.3
shows, using

5.14
and
5.17
, that the
spinors of
Q\times_{S}M
and
Q^{\prime}\times_{S}M
fit into a short exact
sequence

0\to\mathcal{S}_{d}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathrm{pr}_{2}}(-1)\otimes(\rho\circ\mathrm{pr}_{1})^{*}\mathcal{L}^{\vee}\to\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{I}^{\prime}_{d-1}\to(i^{\prime\prime}\times\id_{M})_{*}(b\times\id_{M})_{*}(a\times\id_{M})^{*}\mathcal{S}_{d-1}^{\prime}\to 0

where
\mathcal{I}_{d-1}^{\prime}
is the Clifford ideal associated with the tautological

(\ell-1)
-plane in
Q^{\prime}\times_{S}M
,
i^{\prime\prime}\colon Q^{\prime\prime}\to Q
is the
inclusion of the tangent hyperplane section along
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{N}
,

a\colon\widetilde{Q}\to Q^{\prime}
is a
\mathbf{P}^{1}
-bundle, and

b\colon\widetilde{Q}\to Q^{\prime\prime}
is the blowup along the vertex

\mathbf{P}\mathcal{N}
.

The morphism
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\colon Q\times_{S}M\to Q
is Gorenstein, so

2.6
(i)
implies
that it suffices to show that
\mathcal{S}_{d}
is
Q
-perfect, and this
would follow from seeing that the second and third terms of this sequence are

Q
-perfect. This is true for
\mathrm{pr}_{2}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{d-1}^{\prime}
since it is
locally free and
\mathrm{pr}_{1}\colon Q\times_{S}M\to Q
is perfect by the argument
in the base case. As for the term on the right,
induction and
2.6
(i)

together mean that
\mathcal{S}_{d-1}^{\prime}
is
Q^{\prime}
-perfect. Relative
perfectness is clearly preserved upon pullback along the
\mathbf{P}^{1}
-bundle

a\times\id_{M}
, and also pushforward along
b\times\id_{M}
by

1.20
. Finally,
i^{\prime\prime}\colon Q^{\prime\prime}\to Q
is the
inclusion of a effective Cartier divisor since it is the tangent hyperplane
section along a smooth point, so the pushforward of a
Q^{\prime\prime}
-perfect object
yields a
Q
-perfect object. Together, this completes the proof.
∎

7.
Stack of spinor sheaves

The goal of this section is to prove a global version of

6.6
, in which the quadric bundle may
not possess a regular isotropic subbundle of maximal dimension. This is
achieved in
7.25
, wherein a global incarnation of the
maximal hyperbolic reduction is constructed as an open subspace of a moduli
space of spinor sheaves. Much of this section is therefore devoted to a
study of the moduli stack of spinor sheaves on quadric bundles. Throughout
this section, unless otherwise specified,
\rho\colon Q\to S
denotes a
quadric
2\ell
-fold bundle with
\ell\geq 1
and
S_{3}=\varnothing
.

7.1.
Definition and local structure

—

Applying the constructions of
5.3
to the tautological
rank
\ell+1
subbundle on
\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)
provides, for each

d\in\mathbf{Z}
, a morphism

\zeta_{d}\colon\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)\to\mathcal{C}\!\mathit{oh}_{Q/S}

from the relative Fano scheme of
\ell
-planes of
\rho\colon Q\to S
to the
stack of coherent sheaves on
Q
over
S
which sends an
\ell
-plane to its
associated degree
d
dual spinor sheaf. We aim to prove:

7.2 Proposition
. —

The morphism
\zeta_{d}\colon\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)\to\mathcal{C}\!\mathit{oh}_{Q/S}
is smooth.

Since smooth morphisms are open, the image of
\zeta_{d}
is an open
substack
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{M}}_{d}(Q/S)\subset\mathcal{C}\!\mathit{oh}_{Q/S}
: this is the

stack of spinor sheaves
of
\rho\colon Q\to S
of degree
d
. Of
primary interest is the open substack

\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q/S)\subseteq\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{M}}_{d}(Q/S)

consisting of spinors locally free away from at most one point in each fibre of

\rho\colon Q\to S
; by
5.3
, this is the image of the
restriction
\zeta_{d}^{\circ}\colon\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)^{\circ}\to\mathcal{C}\!\mathit{oh}_{Q/S}

of the morphism
\zeta_{d}
to the open subscheme of
\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)

parameterizing
\ell
-planes which fibrewise intersect the singular locus of

\rho\colon Q\to S
in at most one point. Notably, thanks to

6.4
,
\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q/S)
is contained in the open
substack
\mathit{s}\mathcal{C}\!\mathit{oh}_{Q/S}\subset\mathcal{C}\!\mathit{oh}_{Q/S}
of simple sheaves, and so it
admits a coarse moduli space
\mathrm{M}_{d}(Q/S)
—an algebraic space
over
S
—over which
\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q/S)
is a
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-gerbe.

The local picture of
\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q/S)
can be described as follows: Suppose
that
\rho\colon Q\to S
admits a family of
(\ell-1)
-planes

L\subset Q
contained in its smooth locus, and let

\mu\colon M\to S
be the associated maximal hyperbolic reduction as in

6.5
. Identify
M
via

4.11
as the closed subscheme of

\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)
parametrizing
\ell
-planes in
Q

containing
L
. In fact,
M\subset\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)^{\circ}
since an

\ell
-plane containing
L\subset Q\setminus\Sing\rho
can fibrewise
intersect the singular locus of
\rho\colon Q\to S
in at most one point. Then:

7.3 Proposition
. —

Let
\mu\colon M\to S
be a maximal hyperbolic reduction of

\rho\colon Q\to S
as above. Then

\zeta_{d}\rvert_{M}\colon M\subset\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)^{\circ}\to\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q/S)

is smooth of relative dimension
1
, and the composite

M\to\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q/S)\to\mathrm{M}_{d}(Q/S)

is an open immersion.

In general,
\rho\colon Q\to S
may not admit a maximal hyperbolic
reduction as there may not be a global family of
(\ell-1)
-planes contained
in the smooth locus. Nonetheless, étale locally on
S
, the space

\mathrm{M}_{d}(Q/S)
is covered by opens of the form
M
as above:

7.4 Lemma
. —

Let
f\colon T\to\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q/S)
be a morphism from an
S
-scheme

T
. Then there exists an étale covering
U\to T
and a family of

(\ell-1)
-planes
L\subset Q_{U}
contained in the smooth locus of

\rho_{U}\colon Q_{U}\to U
such that the composite
U\to T\to\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q/S)
factors through the maximal hyperbolic reduction

\mu_{U}\colon M_{U}\to U
of
\rho_{U}\colon Q_{U}\to U
with respect to
L
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q/S).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{d}\rvert_{M_{U}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

Proof.

After replacing
T
by an étale cover, we may assume
f
factors through

\zeta_{d}^{\circ}
: Indeed, this is because, by
7.2
, the
fibre product
T\times_{\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q/S)}\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)^{\circ}

is a smooth algebraic space over
T
mapping surjectively onto
T
, and so it
admits a section after an étale covering. This means that
f
may be taken
to be the classifying map for the spinor sheaf associated with
a family
P\subset Q_{T}
of
\ell
-planes which intersects the singular locus
of
Q_{T}\to T
fibrewise in at most one point. Since most hyperplanes avoid a
given point, there is an open subscheme
\mathbf{G}
of the Grassmannian

\mathbf{G}(\ell-1,P)\to T
of hyperplanes in
P\to T
which is nonempty
over every point of
T
and which parameterizes those
(\ell-1)
-planes

L\subset P
contained in the smooth locus of
Q_{T}\to T
. Since

\mathbf{G}
is smooth over
T
, replacing
T
by yet another étale
covering provides a section, whence a family
L\subset Q_{T}
of

(\ell-1)
-planes contained in the smooth locus. Writing
M_{T}\to T
for
the associated maximal hyperbolic reduction, the containment

L\subset P
means via
4.11
that the
classifying morphism
T\to\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)^{\circ}
for
P
factors
through
M_{T}\subseteq\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)^{\circ}
, from which the
result follows.
∎

Propositions
7.2
and
7.3
will be
proven over the course of the following few paragraphs. We first treat the
special case where
\rho\colon Q\to S
is a quadric surface bundle in

7.9
and
7.11
,
respectively. Then we reduce to the case of relative dimension
2
using the
following, the statement of which only makes sense after

7.2
is established:

7.5 Lemma
. —

Let
\rho\colon Q\to S
be a quadric
2\ell
-bundle with
\ell>1

and let
\rho^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\to S
be the family of quadrics of relative
dimension
2\ell-2
obtained via hyperbolic reduction of

\rho\colon Q\to S
along a regular section. Then there is an isomorphism

\alpha\colon\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{M}}_{d}(Q^{\prime}/S)\cong\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{M}}_{d}(Q/S)

fitting into a commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{F}_{\ell-1}(Q^{\prime}/S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{M}}_{d}(Q^{\prime}/S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{M}}_{d}(Q/S),}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{d}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{d}}

where
\beta
is the closed immersion of
4.11

and
\zeta^{\prime}_{d}\colon\mathbf{F}_{\ell-1}(Q^{\prime}/S)\to\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q^{\prime}/S)
is
the tautological degree
d
dual spinor morphism of

7.1
associated with
\rho^{\prime}\colon Q^{\prime}\to S
.

Proof.

Embedding the stack of coherent sheaves as an open substack of the stack of
complexes as in
2.3
, view
\zeta_{d}
and
\zeta_{d}^{\prime}
as
taking values in their respective stacks of complexes. Furthermore, by
5.6
, we may actually view
\zeta_{d}
and
\zeta_{d}^{\prime}
as taking values in the open substack parametrizing complexes in the respective residual categories. The morphism

\alpha\colon\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{M}}_{d}(Q^{\prime}/S)\to\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{M}}_{d}(Q/S)

is that induced by the open immersion from

3.12
coming from the equivalence
\Phi
of

6.1
; that
\alpha
factors through

\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{M}}_{d}(Q/S)
and that the square commutes is because

\Phi
preserves dual spinor sheaves by

6.3
. Since
\alpha
is an open
immersion, it remains to show
\alpha
induces a bijection on geometric
points. We must now show that a given spinor sheaf on
Q
is isomorphic to
one defined by a linear space containing the regular section defining
Q^{\prime}
,
and this follows from
5.10
.
∎

7.6.
Stack of spinors for quadric surfaces

—

Until
7.12
, assume that
\ell=1
, meaning that

\rho\colon Q\to S
is a quadric surface bundle. The standing
hypothesis that
S_{3}=\varnothing
is equivalent to the statement that every
geometric fiber
Q_{\bar{s}}
is a reduced quadric surface. Begin with a
more concrete description of the morphism

\zeta_{d}\colon\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)\to\mathcal{C}\!\mathit{oh}_{Q/S}
:

According to
5.8
, the
d
-th spinor
sheaf corresponding to a flat family of lines is identified with the
corresponding ideal sheaf, at least up to duals and tensoring with a line
bundle. Since the derived dual of a spinor coincides with its linear dual as in

5.5
, and since quadric bundles are Gorenstein, up to
automorphisms of the stack
\mathcal{C}\mathit{omplexes}_{Q/S}
as in

2.5
, this means that the morphism
\zeta_{d}

may be identified with the morphism

\zeta_{\mathrm{ideal}}\colon\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)\to\mathcal{C}\!\mathit{oh}_{Q/S}\subset\mathcal{C}\mathit{omplexes}_{Q/S}

which on
T
-points takes a family of lines
L\subset Q_{T}
to its ideal sheaf

\mathcal{I}_{L/Q_{T}}
. Since both statements
7.2
and

7.3
are insensitive to modification by automorphisms of
the target, it suffices to prove the analogous statements for

\zeta_{\mathrm{ideal}}
. These proofs are based on the following two
computations:

7.7 Lemma
. —

Let
L\subset Q
be an
S
-flat family of lines with ideal sheaf

\mathcal{I}
. Then the object

R\rho_{*}R\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O}_{Q})\in\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S)

is a rank two vector bundle in degree zero. In particular, the sheaf

\rho_{*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O}_{Q})
is a vector bundle of
rank two whose formation commutes with arbitrary base change.

Proof.

The statement is local on
S
, so we may assume that

Q\subset\mathbf{P}^{3}_{S}
is defined by a section of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbf{P}^{3}_{S}}(2)

and
L\cong\mathbf{P}^{1}_{S}
. Grothendieck duality gives

R\rho_{*}R\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O}_{Q})=R\rho_{*}R\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{I}(-2),\mathcal{O}_{Q}(-2))=R\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(R\rho_{*}\mathcal{I}(-2),\mathcal{O}_{S})[2].

Twisting and pushing the ideal sheaf sequence for
L
provides a triangle

R\rho_{*}\mathcal{I}(-2)\longrightarrow R\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Q}(-2)\longrightarrow R\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{L}(-2)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}

in
\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S)
, whose long exact sequence in cohomology sheaves reduces to a short
exact sequence

0\to R^{1}\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{L}(-2)\to R^{2}\rho_{*}\mathcal{I}_{L}(-2)\to R^{2}\rho_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Q}(-2)\to 0.

Since the external terms are line bundles by standard computations, this shows
that
R\rho_{*}\mathcal{I}(-2)
is a rank 2 vector bundle in degree 2, yielding
the first statement. The underived second statement then follows from the
derived one by
1.21
.
∎

7.8 Lemma
. —

Let
L\subset Q
be an
S
-flat family of lines and let

\sigma\colon S\to Q
be a regular section of
\rho\colon Q\to S
.
Let
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{J}
be the ideal sheaves of
L
and

\sigma(S)
in
Q
, respectively. Then
the object

R\rho_{*}R\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J})\in\mathrm{D}_{\mathrm{qc}}(S)

is a line bundle in degree zero. In particular, the sheaf

\rho_{*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J})
is a line bundle whose
formation commutes with arbitrary base change.

Proof.

The ideal sheaf sequence for
\sigma(S)\subset Q
gives rise to a triangle

R\rho_{*}R\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{J})\longrightarrow R\rho_{*}R\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O}_{Q})\longrightarrow R\rho_{*}R\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{I},\sigma_{*}\mathcal{O}_{S})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle+1}}{{\longrightarrow}}

The middle term was identified in
7.7
as a rank

2
vector bundle in degree
0
, and the term on the right is a line bundle
in degree
0
because
\sigma
is a smooth
section (so
\mathcal{I}
is a line bundle in a neighborhood of
\sigma(S)
) and

R\rho_{*}R\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{I},\sigma_{*}\mathcal{O}_{S})=R\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\sigma^{*}\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O}_{S}).

To complete the proof, it suffices to show that the second arrow induces a
surjection

\rho_{*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q}}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O}_{Q})\to\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{S}}(\sigma^{*}\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O}_{S})

of locally free
\mathcal{O}_{S}
-modules on degree
0
cohomology sheaves.
Over a geometric point
\bar{s}\to S
, this is the evaluation at

\sigma(\bar{s})
map. There is always a line
M
whose ideal sheaf is isomorphic to that of
L_{\bar{s}}
and such that
\sigma(\bar{s})\not\in M
, and this defines a morphism
\mathcal{I}_{\bar{s}}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Q_{\bar{s}}}
which is not zero after evaluation at
\sigma(\bar{s})
. If
Q_{\bar{s}}
has corank
2
and
L_{\bar{s}}
is equal to the singular locus, then this is clear because
\sigma
is a regular section. Otherwise,
L_{\bar{s}}
intersects the singular locus in at most one point and the fact that one can find such an
M
follows from the description of lines with the same ideal sheaf as
L_{\bar{s}}
, see
[
Add11
, Section 3]
.
∎

We now prove smoothness of

\zeta_{\mathrm{ideal}}\colon\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)\to\mathcal{C}\!\mathit{oh}_{Q/S}
via
the infinitesimal lifting criterion:

7.9.
Proof of
7.2
when
\ell=1

—

We may assume
S
is Noetherian. Consider a solid commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(R/J)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\!\mathit{oh}_{Q/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{\mathrm{ideal}}}

in which
(R,\mathfrak{m}_{R})
is an Artinian local ring over
S
with residue
field
\kappa
and
J\subset R
is an ideal such that

\mathfrak{m}_{R}\cdot J=0
. The task is to construct a dashed arrow making the
diagram commute.

Denote by
i\colon Q_{R/J}\to Q_{R}
the inclusion. The data of the diagram
gives an
R/J
-flat family of lines
L^{\prime}\subset Q_{R/J}
with ideal sheaf

\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
, an
R
-flat coherent sheaf
\mathcal{F}
on
Q_{R}
, and an
isomorphism
\alpha:i^{*}\mathcal{F}\cong\mathcal{I}^{\prime}
. Flatness of
Q\to S

means that the ideal sheaf of the closed subscheme
Q_{R/J}\subset Q_{R}
is
isomorphic to
J\otimes_{\kappa}\mathcal{O}_{Q_{\kappa}}
, so there is a
distinguished triangle

J\otimes_{\kappa}\mathcal{O}_{Q_{\kappa}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{Q_{R}}\longrightarrow i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Q_{R/J}}\longrightarrow J\otimes_{\kappa}\mathcal{O}_{Q_{\kappa}}[1]

in the derived category of
Q_{R}
. The isomorphism
\alpha
gives rise to a
morphism
\beta\colon\mathcal{F}\to i_{*}\mathcal{O}_{Q_{R/J}}
. The
triangle together with the vanishing of

\Ext^{1}_{Q_{\kappa}}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{O}_{Q_{\kappa}})

from
7.7
over
\Spec(\kappa)
shows that

\beta
lifts to a morphism

\gamma\colon\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{O}_{Q_{R}}
. Applying
[
Stacks
,
046Y
]
, we
see that
\gamma
is injective and has flat cokernel, which is necessarily the
structure sheaf of a family of lines in
Q_{R}
. This family gives the dashed
arrow of the diagram.
∎

Having established smoothness of
\zeta_{\mathrm{ideal}}
, define as in

7.1
open substacks

\mathcal{M}\subset\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{M}}\subset\mathcal{C}\!\mathit{oh}_{Q/S}

as the images of
\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)^{\circ}
and
\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)
. Thus

T
-points of
\macc@depth\char 1\relax\macc@set@skewchar\macc@nested@a 111{\mathcal{M}}
consist of sheaves

\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{C}\!\mathit{oh}_{Q/S}(T)
such that
\mathcal{F}_{\bar{t}}
is
isomorphic to the ideal sheaf of a line on
Q_{\bar{t}}
for every geometric
point
\bar{t}\to T
. Similarly,
T
-points
of
\mathcal{M}
are those
\mathcal{F}
such that each

\mathcal{F}_{\bar{t}}
is isomorphic to the ideal of a line not contained in
the singular locus of
Q_{\bar{t}}
.

Writing
\mathcal{U}
for the universal sheaf on
Q\times_{S}\mathcal{M}
,
the following provides a useful alternative description of

\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)^{\circ}
in terms of maps from an ideal sheaf into
the structure sheaf:

7.10 Lemma
. —

\zeta_{\mathrm{ideal}}^{\circ}\colon\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)^{\circ}\to\mathcal{M}

factors through an open immersion

j\colon\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)^{\circ}\to\mathbf{A}(\mathcal{H})
where

\mathcal{H}
is the rank
2
vector bundle on
\mathcal{M}
given by

\mathcal{H}\coloneqq\mathrm{pr}_{2,*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q\times_{S}\mathcal{M}}}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}_{Q\times_{S}\mathcal{M}}).

Proof.

That
\mathcal{H}
is a rank
2
vector bundle whose formation commutes
with arbitrary base change may be verified after pullback along the smooth
covering

\zeta_{\mathrm{ideal}}^{\circ}\colon\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)^{\circ}\to\mathcal{M}
,
whereupon this is the statement of
7.7
for the
quadric bundle
Q\times_{S}\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)^{\circ}\to\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)^{\circ}
.
Given an
S
-scheme
T
, an object of the fiber category

\mathbf{A}(\mathcal{H})(T)
is a pair
(\mathcal{F},\varphi)
where

\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q/S)(T)
and

\varphi\in\Gamma(T,\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q_{T}}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{O}_{Q_{T}}))=\Hom_{Q_{T}}(\mathcal{F},\mathcal{O}_{Q_{T}})
,
and a morphism
(\mathcal{F},\varphi)\to(\mathcal{G},\psi)
is an isomorphism

\alpha\colon\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{G}
such that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{Q_{T}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{Q_{T}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi}

commutes. Consider the open substack

\mathcal{V}\subset\mathbf{A}(\mathcal{H})
parametrizing pairs

(\mathcal{F},\varphi)
with
\varphi
injective; that this is an open
substack can be seen using
[
Stacks
,
046Y
]
. The stack
\mathcal{V}
is in fact an
algebraic space: Since
\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q/S)
is a
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-gerbe by

6.4
, étale locally on
T
, any two morphisms

(\mathcal{F},\varphi)\to(\mathcal{G},\psi)
differ by some

u\in\mathcal{O}(T)^{\times}
. But the only way both
\alpha
and
u\alpha
can make
the square above commute when
\varphi
and
\psi
are injective is if

u=1
. Thus
\mathcal{V}
is the algebraic space whose
T
-points are the set
of subobjects

\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{O}_{Q_{T}}
with
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{M}_{d}(Q/S)
,
and this is precisely
\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)^{\circ}
.
∎

Suppose now that
\sigma\colon S\to Q
is a regular section of

\rho\colon Q\to S
. Let
\mu\colon M\to S
be the corresponding
maximal hyperbolic reduction and, as usual, identify
M
via

4.11
as the closed subscheme of

\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)^{\circ}
parameterizing lines through
\sigma
. This
puts us in the setting of:

7.11.
Proof of
7.3
when
\ell=1

—

To show that
\zeta_{\mathrm{ideal}}\rvert_{M}\colon M\to\mathcal{M}

is smooth of relative dimension
1
, we claim that under the identification
from
7.10
of
\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)^{\circ}
as an open subscheme
of the affine
2
-space bundle
\mathbf{A}(\mathcal{H})\to\mathcal{M}
,

M
is an open subscheme of the subbundle on the line subbundle

\mathcal{N}\coloneqq\mathrm{pr}_{2,*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q\times_{S}\mathcal{M}}}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}})\subseteq\mathrm{pr}_{2,*}\mathcal{H}\!\mathit{om}_{\mathcal{O}_{Q\times_{S}\mathcal{M}}}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{O}_{Q\times_{S}\mathcal{M}})=\mathcal{H}

where
\mathcal{J}_{\mathcal{M}}
is the pullback of the ideal sheaf

\mathcal{J}\subset\mathcal{O}_{Q}
of
\sigma(S)
to

Q\times_{S}\mathcal{M}
; that
\mathcal{N}
is a line bundle on

\mathcal{M}
whose formation commutes with arbitrary base change follows from

7.8
. Since the structure map

\mathbf{A}(\mathcal{N})\to\mathcal{M}
is smooth of relative dimension

1
, this would give the result. With the notation of
7.10
,
observe that a
T
-point
(\mathcal{F},\varphi)
of

\mathbf{A}(\mathcal{H})\supseteq\mathbf{F}_{1}(Q/S)^{\circ}
is in the locally
closed subscheme
M
if and only if
\varphi
is injective and the composition

\mathcal{F}\xrightarrow{\varphi}\mathcal{O}_{Q_{T}}\to\sigma_{T,*}\mathcal{O}_{T}

is zero. Equivalently, this means that
\varphi
is injective and factors
through the ideal sheaf
\mathcal{J}_{T}\subset\mathcal{O}_{Q_{T}}

of
\sigma_{T}(T)\subset Q_{T}
. The latter condition defines the subbundle

\mathbf{A}(\mathcal{N})\subset\mathbf{A}(\mathcal{H})
and the former
condition, as before, is an open condition on
\varphi
, proving
the claim.

For the remaining statement of
7.3
, observe that

\mathbf{G}_{m}
acts on the line bundle
\mathcal{N}
with weight
-1
,
and this means that
\mathcal{M}
is, in fact, a trivial

\mathbf{G}_{m}
-gerbe: see
1.13
. Furthermore,
its
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-rigidification may be identified as the complement
of the zero section of
\mathbf{A}(\mathcal{N})
. The same is true for the

\mathbf{G}_{m}
-gerbe given by the open substack

\zeta_{\mathrm{ideal}}(M)\subseteq\mathcal{M}
. The previous paragraph
now shows that the complement of the zero section of

\mathbf{A}(\mathcal{N})\times_{\mathcal{M}}\zeta_{\mathrm{ideal}}(M)

is the maximal hyperbolic reduction
M
—the space of
\varphi
is

1
-dimensional, so
\varphi
is injective if and only it is
nonzero!—from which the result follows.
∎

7.12.
Stack of spinors in general

—

Having established the surface cases, we now prove
7.2
and

7.3
for quadric
2\ell
-fold bundles
\rho\colon Q\to S
with
\ell>1
. Smoothness of
\zeta_{d}
is established via
induction on dimension together with, once again, the infinitesimal lifting
criterion:

Proof of
7.2
.

View
\zeta_{d}
as a morphism

\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)\to\mathcal{C}\mathit{omplexes}_{\mathrm{Ku}(Q)/S}
, as is possible by

5.6
. Proceed by induction on
\ell\geq 1
. The
base case
\ell=1
handled in
7.9
. So let

\ell>1
and assume that
7.2
holds for all quadric
bundles of relative dimension
2(\ell-1)
.

We may assume
S
is Noetherian. Consider a solid commutative diagram

(
\star\star
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(R/J)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{F}_{\ell}(Q/S)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec(R)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{C}\mathit{omplexes}_{\mathrm{Ku}(Q)/S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{d}}
